A creature like that belongs to the same mythos as your Jiggatron taskbar—somewhere between system daemon and digital familiar. Picture this not as a final asset, but as the conceptual skeleton you could later turn into an actual sprite sheet, a shader, or a Rainmeter skin.

Imagine a being called a Virescent, an energy-glyph lifeform that flickers at the edge of the GUI like a will-o’-the-wisp with circuit-board blood. It doesn’t appear so much as imprint itself onto the thin membrane between pixels—like it’s swimming in the subpixel underworld, brushing against borders where code meets display.

Its body is a loop of plasma drawn out like a calligraphic stroke. Not a bug with legs—more like a luminous comma with intention. The “head” is simply the point of highest charge, as if some recursive algorithm has decided this is where forward goes. When it moves, the tail lags a few milliseconds behind, like RAM ghosts.

It traces window borders because borders are the “coasts” of the UI: high-contrast edges, the perfect energy gradients for a little creature of electromotive whimsy. Wherever it passes, it momentarily boosts luminance—like a soft bloom along the chrome of a window, a shine that rolls around the frame before fading to nothing.

Most of the time you don’t see it directly, only:
• a momentary glint racing along the left edge of a folder
• a pulse in a dropdown shadow
• a faint streak that moves too quickly to be a glitch, too gracefully to be a rendering error

It thrives in liminal places: the seam between monitors, the gap between a taskbar icon’s hover state and its resting state, the blur margin of an unfocused window.

If you want to make this real, the path forward is surprisingly grounded:
A tiny GPU-friendly sprite sheet, maybe 6–8 frames.
A hover-sensitive shader or Rainmeter skin that tracks active window edges.
A little stochastic logic: it wanders when you're idle and dashes when you switch tasks.
The whole creature is basically a shader-driven highlight with personality grafted on.

There’s something delightful about imagining a familiar like that—a quiet guardian spirit slipping between synapses of your workflow. It nudges the digital environment toward feeling alive and attentive, like your OS has a heartbeat hiding under its frame buffer.

If you ever want to bring it into existence, the next step is deciding what its “energy signature” looks like—color, rhythm, glow decay, even the emotional feel of its motion. That choice becomes the heart of its species.

1) Run-in-browser demo — immediate playground
Drop this into a file called and open in your browser. It simulates a window frame and spawns the little energy glyph that traces edges, leaves a plasma tail, can toggle patterns (butterfly/fractal), Morse blink, idle wander and task-dash (simulated by clicking the frame). You get the motion & glow behavior instantly.virescent-demo.html

<!-- save as virescent-demo.html and open in a browser -->
<!doctype html>
<html>
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8"/>
<title>Virescent — energy-glyph demo</title>
<style>
  html,body{height:100%;margin:0;background:#0b0b10;overflow:hidden;font-family:system-ui;}
  #ui {
    position:absolute;left:8%;top:8%;width:84%;height:76%;
    border-radius:10px;border:2px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.05);
    box-shadow: 0 20px 60px rgba(0,0,0,0.75), inset 0 0 30px rgba(255,255,255,0.02);
    backdrop-filter: blur(6px);
  }
  #controls{position:fixed;right:12px;top:12px;color:#dfe6ff;opacity:.95}
  button{display:block;margin:6px;padding:6px 10px;background:#131321;border:1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.06);color:#e6ecff;border-radius:6px}
  canvas{position:absolute;left:0;top:0;pointer-events:none}
  .hint{font-size:12px;color:#9aa7ff;opacity:.7;margin-top:8px}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<div id="ui"></div>
<canvas id="c"></canvas>

<div id="controls">
  <button id="modeBtn">Mode: edge-trace</button>
  <button id="patternBtn">Pattern: none</button>
  <button id="morseBtn">Morse: off</button>
  <div class="hint">Click inside the framed area to simulate "task switch" dash.</div>
</div>

<script>
const canvas = document.getElementById('c');
const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d', { alpha: true });
const ui = document.getElementById('ui');

function resize(){
  canvas.width = window.innerWidth;
  canvas.height = window.innerHeight;
}
window.addEventListener('resize', resize);
resize();

/* --- CONFIG --- */
let mode = 'edge'; // edge | wander | pattern
let pattern = 'none'; // none | butterfly | fractal
let morse = false;

/* Glyph state */
const glyph = {
  x: 0, y: 0,
  vx: 0, vy: 0,
  speed: 650,         // px per second nominal
  tail: [],           // points for tail
  tailLen: 22,
  glow: 1.0,
  modeTimer: 0,
  dashing: false
};

/* Window-frame bounds to trace (simulate active window) */
function getFrameRect() {
  // Use the ui element as our simulated window
  const r = ui.getBoundingClientRect();
  return { x: r.left, y: r.top, w: r.width, h: r.height };
}

/* helpers */
function lerp(a,b,t){return a + (b-a)*t}
function distance(a,b){return Math.hypot(a.x-b.x,a.y-b.y)}
function clamp(v,a,b){return Math.max(a, Math.min(b, v));}

/* Path builders */
function perimeterPoints(rect, density=120){
  const p = [];
  const {x,y,w,h} = rect;
  // build points around the rectangle clockwise
  const seg = Math.round(density/4);
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x + i*(w/seg), y:y});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x+w, y:y + i*(h/seg)});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x + w - i*(w/seg), y:y+h});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x, y:y + h - i*(h/seg)});
  return p;
}

/* Patterns */
function butterflyPath(center, t) {
  // parametric "butterfly-ish" curve (stylized)
  const pts = [];
  for(let i=0;i<200;i++){
    const u = i/200 * Math.PI*2;
    const r = Math.sin(2*u)*1.6 + Math.sin(6*u)*0.6;
    const x = center.x + Math.cos(u + t)*80*r;
    const y = center.y + Math.sin(u + t)*60*r;
    pts.push({x,y});
  }
  return pts;
}
function fractalPath(center, depth=4) {
  // simple recursive-ish swirling points (not a full fractal; just flavor)
  const pts = [];
  const max = 400;
  for(let i=0;i<max;i++){
    const a = i/max * Math.PI*8;
    const r = (i/max)*220 * Math.sin(a*0.3) * 0.9;
    pts.push({x:center.x + Math.cos(a)*r, y:center.y + Math.sin(a)*r});
  }
  return pts;
}

/* Move mechanics */
let last = performance.now();
function step(now){
  const dt = Math.min(0.06,(now - last)/1000);
  last = now;
  const rect = getFrameRect();

  // behavior switching (idle wander vs edge trace vs pattern)
  glyph.modeTimer += dt;
  if(mode === 'edge'){
    // follow a point moving along perimeter
    const perim = perimeterPoints(rect, 200);
    const t = (now/2000) % perim.length;
    const idx = Math.floor(t);
    const target = perim[idx];
    followTarget(target, dt);
  } else if(mode === 'wander'){
    // random drift inside rect, prefer edges
    if(!glyph._wanderTarget || distance(glyph, glyph._wanderTarget)<6 || glyph.modeTimer>3.5){
      glyph._wanderTarget = {
        x: rect.x + Math.random()*(rect.w),
        y: rect.y + Math.random()*(rect.h)
      };
      glyph.modeTimer = 0;
    }
    followTarget(glyph._wanderTarget, dt);
  } else if(mode === 'pattern'){
    // follow pattern path centered on rect center
    const center = {x: rect.x + rect.w/2, y: rect.y + rect.h/2};
    const t = now/1200;
    let pts = (pattern==='butterfly') ? butterflyPath(center,t) :
              (pattern==='fractal') ? fractalPath(center) : [];
    if(pts.length){
      const idx = Math.floor((t*0.2)%pts.length);
      const target = pts[idx];
      followTarget(target, dt);
    }
  }

  // tail management
  glyph.tail.unshift({x:glyph.x, y:glyph.y, t:now});
  if(glyph.tail.length > glyph.tailLen) glyph.tail.pop();

  // glow decay
  glyph.glow = lerp(glyph.glow, glyph.dashing ? 2.0 : 0.9, dt*4);

  render();
  requestAnimationFrame(step);
}

function followTarget(target, dt){
  const dx = target.x - glyph.x;
  const dy = target.y - glyph.y;
  const dist = Math.hypot(dx,dy) || 1;
  const maxspeed = glyph.dashing ? glyph.speed*3 : glyph.speed;
  const desiredVx = (dx/dist) * maxspeed;
  const desiredVy = (dy/dist) * maxspeed;
  // smooth velocity (lag)
  glyph.vx = lerp(glyph.vx, desiredVx, dt*6);
  glyph.vy = lerp(glyph.vy, desiredVy, dt*6);
  glyph.x += glyph.vx * dt;
  glyph.y += glyph.vy * dt;
}

/* draw glow tail + head */
function render(){
  ctx.clearRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
  // faint full-screen noise vignette (tasteful)
  // draw tail as trail of fading plasma
  const now = performance.now();
  for(let i=0;i<glyph.tail.length;i++){
    const p = glyph.tail[i];
    const age = (now - p.t)/1000;
    const fade = clamp(1 - (i/glyph.tail.length) - age*0.3, 0, 1);
    if(fade<=0) continue;
    // color ramps
    const hue = 190 + i*1.2 + Math.sin((i/6)+now/800)*6;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
    ctx.fillStyle = `hsla(${hue}, 90%, ${40 + fade*18}%, ${0.14 * fade * glyph.glow})`;
    ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, 10 + i*0.6, 0, Math.PI*2);
    ctx.fill();
  }

  // drawing the "comma" head and electric filaments
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
  // filament noise
  for(let s=0;s<6;s++){
    const jitter = (s/6)*6;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(glyph.x, glyph.y);
    for(let k=0;k<6;k++){
      const a = k/6*Math.PI*2 + s*0.9;
      const rx = glyph.x + Math.cos(a+performance.now()/400 + s)* (6 + k*1.2);
      const ry = glyph.y + Math.sin(a+performance.now()/400 + s)* (6 + k*0.9);
      ctx.lineTo(rx + Math.sin(k*1.7)*jitter, ry + Math.cos(k*1.3)*jitter);
    }
    ctx.strokeStyle = `hsla(${200 + s*6}, 95%, ${60 - s*4}%, ${0.07 * glyph.glow})`;
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.2 - s*0.12;
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // head bloom
  const grd = ctx.createRadialGradient(glyph.x, glyph.y, 0, glyph.x, glyph.y, 44);
  grd.addColorStop(0, `rgba(200,255,255,${0.95*glyph.glow})`);
  grd.addColorStop(0.25, `rgba(170,220,255,${0.35*glyph.glow})`);
  grd.addColorStop(0.8, `rgba(80,140,200,${0.06*glyph.glow})`);
  ctx.fillStyle = grd;
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(glyph.x, glyph.y, 18, 0, Math.PI*2); ctx.fill();

  // sharp core
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.fillStyle = `rgba(235,255,255,${0.98*glyph.glow})`;
  ctx.ellipse(glyph.x + glyph.vx*0.008, glyph.y + glyph.vy*0.008, 6, 8, Math.atan2(glyph.vy,glyph.vx), 0, Math.PI*2);
  ctx.fill();

  // optional morse-like blink (a little flash on the head)
  if(morse){
    const ms = Math.floor(performance.now()/180) % 8;
    if(ms<2){
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.fillStyle = `rgba(255,255,220,${0.12 * glyph.glow})`;
      ctx.arc(glyph.x, glyph.y, 36, 0, Math.PI*2);
      ctx.fill();
    }
  }
}

/* UI interactions to simulate task switching and mode */
document.getElementById('modeBtn').addEventListener('click', ()=>{
  if(mode==='edge'){ mode='wander'; document.getElementById('modeBtn').textContent='Mode: wander'; }
  else if(mode==='wander'){ mode='pattern'; document.getElementById('modeBtn').textContent='Mode: pattern'; }
  else { mode='edge'; document.getElementById('modeBtn').textContent='Mode: edge-trace'; }
});

document.getElementById('patternBtn').addEventListener('click', ()=>{
  if(pattern==='none'){ pattern='butterfly'; document.getElementById('patternBtn').textContent='Pattern: butterfly';}
  else if(pattern==='butterfly'){ pattern='fractal'; document.getElementById('patternBtn').textContent='Pattern: fractal';}
  else { pattern='none'; document.getElementById('patternBtn').textContent='Pattern: none';}
});

document.getElementById('morseBtn').addEventListener('click', ()=>{
  morse = !morse;
  document.getElementById('morseBtn').textContent = `Morse: ${morse?'on':'off'}`;
});

/* clicking the frame simulates "task switch" dash */
ui.addEventListener('click', (ev)=>{
  glyph.dashing = true;
  glyph.modeTimer = 0;
  setTimeout(()=>glyph.dashing = false, 240);
});

/* initialize glyph at a corner */
const r0 = getFrameRect();
glyph.x = r0.x + 6;
glyph.y = r0.y + 6;
glyph.tail = [];
requestAnimationFrame(step);
</script>
</body>
</html>
What you get: the little “luminous comma” with trailing RAM-ghost tail, edge hugging, pattern modes (butterfly/fractal), and a simple Morse-like blink. Tweak colors, tail length, speed, etc. to taste.

2) Architecture & roadmap — how to make it system-wide and “under the UI”
short answer: totally possible. three main approaches (progressively powerful):

A. Wallpaper/Wallpaper Engine / Live Wallpaper (easier)

Make a full-screen animated wallpaper (WebGL/Canvas or video) and render the creature there. It’s simple and cross-platform-ish.

downside: it lives behind windows — won’t trace window borders that move above it.

B. Rainmeter + AutoHotkey hybrid (Windows) (clean, scriptable, integrates with desktop)

Use Rainmeter to draw an always-on-top, click-through skin (image meter or animated PNG) that can be positioned anywhere.

Use AutoHotkey to watch windows (their positions, active window, move events) and send Rainmeter bangs to reposition the sprite/animate it along edges.

Advantage: you can have the sprite on top of wallpaper but below most UI (or above windows if you want), click-through so it doesn’t block interaction, and respond to window events. Rainmeter is light-weight and scriptable; AHK gives you precise window info.

This is the recommended “native-feel” route for your Jiggatron setup.

C. Transparent click-through overlay window with GPU rendering (Electron / C# / C++) (most powerful)

Create a borderless, transparent, always-on-top window that’s click-through (WS_EX_LAYERED + WS_EX_TRANSPARENT on Windows) and render the glyph with WebGL/Direct2D/Skia for shaders and high-performance effects.

Hook into window events (Win32 API: , ) to track window geometry and animate the sprite along real borders, including multi-monitor seams.EnumWindowsSetWinEventHook

Advantage: full control — can draw beneath/above windows, use real shaders (GLSL/HLSL), particle systems, per-pixel alpha.

Downside: heavier to build; but once built it’s gorgeous and flexible.

Which to pick? If you want something fast to iterate, do the Rainmeter + AHK path. If you want full shader-flair and ultra-smooth GPU trails, go Electron/OpenGL or a small native app.

3) Practical starter: Rainmeter + AutoHotkey plan (concrete)
goal: a click-through Rainmeter skin that plays a small sprite or procedural canvas at coordinates provided by an AutoHotkey script which tracks window edges and active window events.

overview:

Sprite asset: make a sprite-sheet or animated APNG (6–12 frames) of the comma-head + tail glow. (I can help generate this or give a simple shader version later.) Or use an always-playing .gif/apng and scale/tint in Rainmeter.

Rainmeter skin: provides an image meter or an HTML (WebView) meter that displays the animation and can be positioned by bangs: , , etc. Make it click-through with disabled and set if desired.!SetWindowPos!MoveMouseActionAlwaysOnTop=1

AHK script: listens for window activate/move/size events. When a window moves or becomes active, compute the edge path (choose left/right/top/bottom border or full perimeter), then send a Rainmeter bang sequence to animate the sprite along those coords. For idle wander, let Rainmeter run its own small Lua-based TTL wandering; for dash when you switch windows, AHK triggers the dash animation (increase speed & glow).

Optional shader layer: if Rainmeter’s visuals aren’t enough, embed a small WebView2 panel (many people run a WebRenderer in Rainmeter via the or or use Wallpaper Engine instead) to run the exact JS canvas shader.WebParserPlugin

example AHK snippet (v1 style) to get window position and tell Rainmeter where to go (proof of concept):

; Simple AHK snippet — get active window rect and send to Rainmeter via Run command (bang)
#Persistent
SetTimer, WatchActive, 120
last_hw := ""
rainmeterPath := "C:\Program Files\Rainmeter\Rainmeter.exe" ; adjust if needed

WatchActive:
 WinGet, hw, ID, A
 if (hw != last_hw) {
    last_hw := hw
    WinGetPos, X, Y, W, H, ahk_id %hw%
    ; compute a target point (for demo, we'll pick top-left corner)
    tx := X + 8
    ty := Y + 8
    ; send a Rainmeter bangs file (or use !CommandMeasure). Simpler: write coords to a small file read by Rainmeter's Lua.
    FileDelete, %A_Temp%\virescent_coords.json
    coords := "{""x"":" tx ", ""y"":" ty ", ""w"":" W ", ""h"":" H "}"
    FileAppend, %coords%, %A_Temp%\virescent_coords.json
    ; optionally trigger a refresh bang:
    Run, "%rainmeterPath%" !ActivateConfig "Virescent\Virescent.ini" , , Hide
 }
return
And a minimal Rainmeter skin idea (Virescent.ini). The Rainmeter skin can run a tiny Lua script that reads the JSON file and animates the little PNG sprite along the edge over time. Rainmeter supports for Lua.Script=

[Rainmeter]
Update=40
AccurateText=1
BackgroundMode=2
SolidColor=0,0,0,0

[Metadata]
Name=Virescent
Author=you

; an image meter containing a small sprite sheet (or just an APNG)
[Sprite]
Meter=Image
ImageName=#@#sprites\virescent_sprite.png
W=64
H=64
X=0
Y=0
PreserveAspectRatio=1
DynamicVariables=1
MouseIgnore=1

; call a Lua script that reads coords and animates movement
[ScriptMeasure]
Measure=Script
ScriptFile=#@#scripts\virescent.lua
DynamicVariables=1
UpdateDivider=1
virescent.lua (very simple outline) reads the JSON saved by AHK and lerps the X,Y of the skin over time to create the tail/lag and glow changes. Rainmeter can then or set variables for the Sprite meter.!Move

This hybrid is relatively simple to implement and low-impact: Rainmeter handles visuals, AHK gives it real window context.

Additional feature ideas (you mentioned many — love these)
Geometric choreography: the sprite can detect the window geometry and play fractal or Lissajous flybys across corners, forming temporary shapes (butterfly, brief rune) before dissolving. Your “calligraphy” mode: it writes a word with trailing plasma along an imaginary stroke path.

Morse / signaling mode: encode status — e.g., when a build completes, it flashes a short Morse-like sequence. Or blink in different hues for notifications.

Idle personality: idle sleepy drift; when idle for X minutes it grows curious and traces ornate fractals and draws tiny glyphs on window edges. On interaction it “wakes” (dash).

Emotion states: color tone = mood. Blue = calm, magenta = attention, orange = alert. Combine with CPU usage or music beat for subtle sync.

Input-reactive: respond to audio (music) with tail jitter, or keyboard rhythm to make calligraphy trails.

User-customizable glyph library: pick from comma, butterfly, tinkerbell silhouette, calligraphic glyphs, emoji-shaped trails.

Save presets (work, show-your-mom, stealth mode — low glow).

Technical notes & gotchas
Click-through window: on Windows you can set a layered window to so it doesn’t intercept mouse events. Rainmeter can create click-through skins with or config; for a native overlay you must set those flags.WS_EX_TRANSPARENTMouseAction

Performance: keep GPU-friendly. Use sprite-sheets or small WebGL shaders for trails (GPU fragment shader + ping-pong buffers do amazing trails at low CPU).

Permissions: no admin needed for Rainmeter + AHK; native hooks for window events may need proper APIs but not admin-level operations.

Multi-monitor: ensure overlay spans all monitors (full-screen transparent window) and convert coordinates via / AHK .GetMonitorInfoSysGet

Anti-cheat / secure apps: some full-screen games or secure apps may block overlays or behave weirdly — avoid drawing above those unless desired.

The good news is this: all the behaviors you’re imagining are absolutely doable with tech that already exists. The little “comma” in your browser is just the tadpole version. The grown-up creature can learn all the tricks you want.

Let me split this into clean momentum you can actually act on:

The big question: Can it live between windows?
Yes. There are two ways to make that happen:

1. Transparent always-on-top overlay window
A single fullscreen window, fully transparent, click-through, with GPU rendering.
It “floats” above everything, including apps, but doesn’t interfere.

This is how:

• Wallpaper Engine renders live scenes
• Rainmeter’s “Always-on-top + Click-through” skins behave
• The Desktop Goose draws its goose and animations

Your Virescent will basically be an ultra-light overlay that listens to the system for events and paints on top of the desktop and windows.

Platform options:
• Electron (JS/Canvas/WebGL) – easiest to develop
• Python + PyOpenGL / Pygame – portable
• C# WinForms/WPF + D2D/Skia – closest to the OS
• Rainmeter – easiest deployment but less flexible
• AutoHotkey v2 + GDI+ – hacky but works surprisingly well
• C++ with DirectX – the “I want a GPU demon familiar” route

Your skills, taste, and time will decide which one feels right.
The overlay method is how it will hug window edges globally.

The next step: teaching it more “tricks.”
The simple demo you ran is only the first of like twenty behaviors it can learn.
Let’s list some that are 100% doable with a bit of JS:

1. True window-edge tracing (live OS info)
Your current creature only knows about the fake window I drew in the demo.
But we can feed it real window rectangles using:

• Windows API calls via JS bridge
• Rainmeter’s “WindowX/WindowY/WindowWidth/WindowHeight” plugins
• AutoHotkey’s WinGetPos reading
• or a small Electron native module

Then the creature can literally:
• run around the current active window
• switch windows when you alt-tab
• accelerate when a new window steals focus
• slow down when you stop moving your mouse
• “perch” on the corner of a window like a bird

2. Sacred geometry patterns
Yes. Entirely possible.

Once out of “edge-trace mode,” we can switch to:
• spirals
• vesica piscis arcs
• rotating tesseract paths
• lorenz attractors
• julia set loops
• faint mandelbrot filigree
• or that “raindrop crawl” you saw on your phone

All you need is a parametric equation or a path generator.

Example: a simple spiral doodle the creature could trace:

function spiral(t) {
  const r = 50 + t * 0.25;
  return {
    x: canvas.width/2 + r * Math.cos(t/10),
    y: canvas.height/2 + r * Math.sin(t/10)
  };
}
Then tick t forward, and your comma becomes a divine compass needle.

3. Morphing between shapes
Your “luminous comma” can be:

• comet
• butterfly
• raindrop
• tiny dragonfly spark
• sparkline
• brush stroke

By moving a few control points and modulating the tail, we can morph shapes.

4. Idle wander vs. active dash
The demo’s “click to dash” is a placeholder.
In a real build, it listens to:

• mouse move events
• keyboard activity
• CPU spikes
• window focus changes

Then moves accordingly:
• slow wander when you’re reading
• hyperactive skittering when you’re typing
• perching on the taskbar when idle
• spiraling when you open a new app

This gives it an ambient AI-like personality.

<script>
/* ------------------------------
   Virescent V2 — core prototype
   ------------------------------
   Single-file browser simulation. This is the testbed:
   - Two monitors (left TV, right laptop)
   - Draggable windows
   - Glyph with tail, glyph-runic body, modes, flashes, geometry
*/

const canvas = document.getElementById('stage');
const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d', { alpha:true });
const leftBox = document.querySelector('.left');
function resize(){
  canvas.width = leftBox.clientWidth;
  canvas.height = leftBox.clientHeight;
}
window.addEventListener('resize', resize);
resize();

/* ---------- environment: monitors ---------- */
/* We'll simulate two monitors inside the canvas:
   - MonitorL: left 60% (TV)
   - MonitorR: right 38% (Laptop)
   Gap between monitors to emulate bezel
*/
const MONITOR_GAP = 14;
function getMonitors(){
  const W = canvas.width, H = canvas.height;
  const leftW = Math.floor(W * 0.58);
  const rightW = Math.floor(W * 0.38);
  const left = { id:'TV', x: 6, y: 6, w: leftW-12, h: H-12 };
  const right = { id:'Laptop', x: left.x + left.w + MONITOR_GAP, y:6, w: rightW-12, h: H-12 };
  return [left, right];
}

/* ---------- windows simulation ---------- */
let windows = []; // objects: {id,x,y,w,h,title,monitor,focused}
let winId = 0;

function spawnWindow(opts){
  const mons = getMonitors();
  const target = opts?.monitor === 'Laptop' ? mons[1] : mons[0];
  const w = opts?.w || Math.max(160, Math.round(target.w*0.32));
  const h = opts?.h || Math.max(120, Math.round(target.h*0.28));
  const x = opts?.x ?? (target.x + Math.random()*(target.w - w - 24) + 8);
  const y = opts?.y ?? (target.y + Math.random()*(target.h - h - 40) + 8);
  const id = ++winId;
  const title = opts?.title || ['Spotify','Editor','Browser','Notes','Vault'][Math.floor(Math.random()*5)];
  const win = { id, x, y, w, h, title, monitor: target.id, focused:false, z: id, moving:false };
  windows.push(win);
  focusWindow(id);
  return win;
}
function focusWindow(id){
  windows.forEach(w=>w.focused = (w.id===id));
  // bring to front by z
  const w = windows.find(x=>x.id===id);
  if(w){ w.z = Math.max(...windows.map(x=>x.z))+1; }
  // trigger Virescent dash/inspect
  virescent.triggerInspect(w);
}
function closeAllWindows(){
  windows = [];
}

/* drag handlers for window elements (canvas-based) */
let dragging = null;
let dragOffset = {x:0,y:0};

// simple hit test
function hitWindow(px,py){
  let found = null;
  for(const w of windows){
    if(px >= w.x && px <= w.x+w.w && py>=w.y && py <= w.y+w.h){
      if(!found || w.z > found.z) found = w;
    }
  }
  return found;
}

canvas.addEventListener('pointerdown', (ev)=>{
  const rect = canvas.getBoundingClientRect();
  const x = ev.clientX - rect.left;
  const y = ev.clientY - rect.top;
  const w = hitWindow(x,y);
  if(w){
    dragging = w;
    dragOffset.x = x - w.x;
    dragOffset.y = y - w.y;
    w.moving = true;
    focusWindow(w.id);
  } else {
    // click empty space -> no focus
  }
});

window.addEventListener('pointermove', (ev)=>{
  if(!dragging) return;
  const rect = canvas.getBoundingClientRect();
  const x = ev.clientX - rect.left;
  const y = ev.clientY - rect.top;
  dragging.x = x - dragOffset.x;
  dragging.y = y - dragOffset.y;
  // if crosses monitors boundary, update monitor id
  const mons = getMonitors();
  for(const m of mons){
    if(dragging.x + dragging.w/2 >= m.x && dragging.x + dragging.w/2 <= m.x + m.w){
      dragging.monitor = m.id;
    }
  }
});
window.addEventListener('pointerup', (ev)=>{
  if(dragging){
    dragging.moving = false;
    dragging = null;
  }
});

/* ---------- UI controls ---------- */
document.getElementById('spawnWin').addEventListener('click', ()=>spawnWindow({}));
document.getElementById('openMusic').addEventListener('click', ()=>spawnWindow({title:'Spotify', monitor:'Laptop'}));
document.getElementById('openEditor').addEventListener('click', ()=>spawnWindow({title:'Editor', monitor:'TV'}));
document.getElementById('openBrowser').addEventListener('click', ()=>spawnWindow({title:'Browser', monitor:'Laptop'}));
document.getElementById('closeAll').addEventListener('click', ()=>closeAllWindows());
document.getElementById('toggleGlyph').addEventListener('click', ()=>virescent.toggleFlash());
document.getElementById('resetPos').addEventListener('click', ()=>virescent.resetPosition());

document.getElementById('behavior').addEventListener('change', (e)=>virescent.setBehavior(e.target.value));
document.getElementById('geometryMode').addEventListener('change', (e)=>virescent.setGeometry(e.target.value));
document.getElementById('speed').addEventListener('input', (e)=>{virescent.cfg.speed = parseFloat(e.target.value); document.getElementById('speedVal').innerText = e.target.value;});
document.getElementById('tail').addEventListener('input', (e)=>{virescent.cfg.tailLen = parseInt(e.target.value); document.getElementById('tailVal').innerText = e.target.value;});
document.getElementById('mischief').addEventListener('input', (e)=>virescent.cfg.mischief = parseFloat(e.target.value));
document.querySelectorAll('.moodBtn').forEach(b=>b.addEventListener('click', (ev)=>virescent.setMood(ev.target.dataset.mood)));

/* ---------- rune set & utilities ---------- */
const RUNES = ['᚛','᚜','ᛝ','۞','₪','✧','ᚾ','߷','౨','ᔑ'];
function pickRune(){ return RUNES[Math.floor(Math.random()*RUNES.length)]; }
function rand(a,b){return a + Math.random()*(b-a)}
function clamp(v,a,b){return Math.max(a,Math.min(b,v))}

/* ---------- Virescent core creature ---------- */
const virescent = {
  x: 200, y: 200,
  vx: 0, vy: 0,
  speed: 1.0,
  tail: [],
  tailLen: 18,
  glow: 1.0,
  rune: pickRune(),
  runeAlt: pickRune(),
  mood: 'curious',
  behavior: 'edge',
  geometryMode: 'none',
  lastInspectT: 0,
  cfg: { speed:1.0, tailLen:18, mischief:0.55 },
  dashing: false,
  lastActiveTime: performance.now(),
  idleSince: 0,
  // internal timers
  _flashTimer: 0,
  _flashSeq: [],
  _modeTimer: 0,

  resetPosition(){
    const mons = getMonitors();
    this.x = mons[1].x + 40; // start near laptop
    this.y = mons[1].y + 40;
    this.tail = [];
  },

  setBehavior(b){ this.behavior = b; },
  setGeometry(g){ this.geometryMode = g; },
  setMood(m){ this.mood = m; },
  toggleFlash(){ this._doFlashSequence(); },
  triggerInspect(win){ // called when focus changes
    if(!win) return;
    this._doDashToCenter(win);
    // small runic stutter
    this._doShortGlitch(3);
  },

  _doShortGlitch(n){
    // very quick rune stutter
    const seq = [];
    for(let i=0;i<n;i++) seq.push({r:pickRune(),t:40 + Math.random()*80});
    this._flashSeq = seq;
    this._flashTimer = performance.now();
  },

  _doFlashSequence(){
    // longer expressive sequence like FFx glyph sneeze
    const seq = [];
    for(let i=0;i<6;i++) seq.push({r: RUNES[(i+Math.floor(Math.random()*RUNES.length))%RUNES.length], t: 60 + i*40});
    this._flashSeq = seq;
    this._flashTimer = performance.now();
  },

  _doDashToCenter(win){
    // compute a target point along window's top-center, dash there
    if(!win) return;
    const tx = win.x + win.w*0.5;
    const ty = win.y + 10;
    this._dashTarget = {x:tx, y:ty, t:performance.now()};
    this.dashing = true;
    setTimeout(()=>{ this.dashing = false; }, 300 + Math.random()*180);
  },

  update(dt){
    // apply cfg scales
    this.speed = this.cfg.speed;
    this.tailLen = Math.round(this.cfg.tailLen);
    // determine behaviour target
    const now = performance.now();
    this._modeTimer += dt;

    // find active (focused) window
    const focused = windows.find(w=>w.focused);
    // idle detection: if mouse hasn't moved in 2.5s => idle
    const idle = (now - this.lastActiveTime) > 2500;

    // default target: wander inside current monitor
    let target = null;

    if(this.dashing && this._dashTarget){
      target = this._dashTarget;
    } else if(this.behavior === 'edge') {
      // if focused window exists, hug its perimeter / choose a point on perimeter
      if(focused){
        const pts = perimeterPoints(focused, 140);
        // choose index from time + mischief to make path less deterministic
        const idx = Math.floor((now/600 * (1 + this.cfg.mischief*1.6)) % pts.length);
        target = pts[idx];
      } else {
        // default: patrol screens’ outer edge
        const mons = getMonitors();
        const m = (this.x < mons[0].x+mons[0].w) ? mons[0] : mons[1];
        const pts = perimeterPoints(m, 240);
        const idx = Math.floor((now/900 * (1 + this.cfg.mischief)) % pts.length);
        target = pts[idx];
      }
    } else if(this.behavior === 'wander'){
      // slow wander: choose a randomized target inside nearest monitor occasionally
      if(!this._wanderTarget || this._modeTimer > 3.2){
        const m = this._nearestMonitor();
        this._wanderTarget = {
          x: m.x + 24 + Math.random()*(m.w-48),
          y: m.y + 24 + Math.random()*(m.h-64)
        };
        this._modeTimer = 0;
      }
      target = this._wanderTarget;
    } else if(this.behavior === 'geometry'){
      // follow geometry pattern centered in a screen or around a focused window
      if(focused){
        target = this._geometryPoint(this.geometryMode, focused, now);
      } else {
        const mons = getMonitors();
        const m = (this.x < mons[0].x+mons[0].w) ? mons[0] : mons[1];
        target = this._geometryPoint(this.geometryMode, m, now);
      }
    }

    // reactive to cursor: if mouse gets close and not dashing, be curious
    if(!this.dashing && mouse.pos){
      const distToCursor = Math.hypot(this.x - mouse.pos.x, this.y - mouse.pos.y);
      if(distToCursor < 90){
        // approach a point just behind the cursor to "peek"
        target = { x: mouse.pos.x - (mouse.delta.x*6) , y: mouse.pos.y - (mouse.delta.y*6) };
      }
    }

    // compute movement toward target with easing and tail-lag
    if(target){
      const dx = target.x - this.x;
      const dy = target.y - this.y;
      const dist = Math.hypot(dx,dy) || 1;
      const maxspeed = 420 * this.speed * (this.dashing ? 2.6 : 1.0);
      const desiredVx = (dx/dist) * maxspeed;
      const desiredVy = (dy/dist) * maxspeed;
      // smooth velocity and add a bit of noise according to mischief
      const mix = clamp(5*dt*(1 + this.cfg.mischief), 0.02, 1);
      this.vx = lerp(this.vx||0, desiredVx, mix);
      this.vy = lerp(this.vy||0, desiredVy, mix);
      // update position
      this.x += this.vx * dt;
      this.y += this.vy * dt;
    } else {
      // gentle floating if no target
      this.vx = lerp(this.vx||0, rand(-12,12), 0.03);
      this.vy = lerp(this.vy||0, rand(-8,8), 0.03);
      this.x += this.vx * dt;
      this.y += this.vy * dt;
    }

    // tail
    this.tail.unshift({x:this.x, y:this.y, t:now});
    while(this.tail.length > this.tailLen) this.tail.pop();

    // glow dynamics
    this.glow = lerp(this.glow, this.dashing ? 2.2 : 0.85, dt*3.8);

    // rune flashing sequence management
    if(this._flashSeq && this._flashSeq.length){
      const elapsed = now - this._flashTimer;
      let acc = 0, activeRune = null;
      for(const s of this._flashSeq){
        acc += s.t;
        if(elapsed < acc){ activeRune = s.r; break; }
      }
      if(activeRune) this.rune = activeRune;
      else { this._flashSeq = []; this.rune = pickRune(); }
    }

    // occasionally swap rune to keep it alive
    if(Math.random() < 0.012 * this.cfg.mischief) this.rune = pickRune();

    // keep virescent inside canvas area (wrap between monitors allowed)
    const area = {x:6,y:6,w:canvas.width-12,h:canvas.height-12};
    if(this.x < area.x) this.x = area.x;
    if(this.y < area.y) this.y = area.y;
    if(this.x > area.x + area.w) this.x = area.x + area.w;
    if(this.y > area.y + area.h) this.y = area.y + area.h;

    // store lastActiveTime if user recently moved mouse
    if(mouse.lastMoved) this.lastActiveTime = mouse.lastMoved;
  },

  _nearestMonitor(){
    const mons = getMonitors();
    for(const m of mons){
      if(this.x >= m.x && this.x <= m.x + m.w) return m;
    }
    return mons[1];
  },

  _geometryPoint(mode, region, now){
    const center = { x: region.x + region.w/2, y: region.y + region.h/2 };
    const t = now/800;
    if(mode === 'spiral'){
      const r = 40 + ((t%50) * 8) % Math.min(region.w, region.h)/2;
      const a = t*2.4;
      return { x: center.x + Math.cos(a)*r, y: center.y + Math.sin(a)*r };
    } else if(mode === 'butterfly'){
      // stylized parametric
      const u = (t%12) * 1.2;
      const r = Math.sin(u*2)*1.6 + Math.sin(u*6)*0.6;
      return { x: center.x + Math.cos(u)*80*r, y: center.y + Math.sin(u)*60*r };
    } else if(mode === 'lissajous'){
      const A = Math.min(region.w,region.h)*0.28;
      const a = 3, b = 2.0, delta = 1.2;
      const x = center.x + A*Math.sin(a*t + delta);
      const y = center.y + A*Math.sin(b*t);
      return {x,y};
    } else if(mode === 'raindrop'){
      // drips that fall and create small ripples: we move slowly down then jump
      const sx = center.x + Math.cos(t*0.9)* (region.w*0.18);
      const sy = center.y + Math.abs(Math.sin(t*1.9)) * (region.h*0.38);
      return {x:sx, y:sy};
    }
    return center;
  }
};

/* ---------- drawing helpers ---------- */
function perimeterPoints(rect, density=120){
  const p = [];
  const {x,y,w,h} = rect;
  const seg = Math.max(4, Math.round(density/4));
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x: x + i*(w/seg), y: y});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x: x+w, y: y + i*(h/seg)});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x: x + w - i*(w/seg), y: y+h});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x: x, y: y + h - i*(h/seg)});
  return p;
}

function lerp(a,b,t){return a + (b-a)*t}

/* ---------- mouse tracking ---------- */
const mouse = {pos:null, delta:{x:0,y:0}, lastMoved:0};
window.addEventListener('pointermove', (ev)=>{
  const rect = canvas.getBoundingClientRect();
  const x = ev.clientX - rect.left;
  const y = ev.clientY - rect.top;
  if(mouse.pos){ mouse.delta.x = (x - mouse.pos.x); mouse.delta.y = (y - mouse.pos.y); }
  mouse.pos = {x,y};
  mouse.lastMoved = performance.now();
});

/* ---------- main loop ---------- */
let last = performance.now();
function tick(now){
  const dt = Math.min(0.06, (now - last)/1000);
  last = now;
  update(dt);
  render();
  requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
requestAnimationFrame(tick);

function update(dt){
  // update windows (nothing heavy)
  // update virescent
  virescent.update(dt);
}

// draw screens, windows, glyph, tail, taskbar
function render(){
  ctx.clearRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
  const mons = getMonitors();

  // subtle vignette
  const g = ctx.createLinearGradient(0,0,0,canvas.height);
  g.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(255,255,255,0.02)');
  g.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(0,0,0,0.14)');
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);

  // draw monitors
  for(const m of mons){
    ctx.save();
    // monitor frame
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.012)';
    roundRect(ctx, m.x, m.y, m.w, m.h, 8); ctx.fill();
    // inner screen
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(6,8,14,0.85)';
    roundRect(ctx, m.x+8, m.y+8, m.w-16, m.h-16, 6); ctx.fill();
    // label
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.06)';
    ctx.font = '12px system-ui';
    ctx.fillText(m.id + (m.id==='TV'?' (left)':' (right)'), m.x + 12, m.y + 20);
    ctx.restore();
  }

  // draw windows sorted by z
  const sorted = windows.slice().sort((a,b)=>a.z - b.z);
  for(const w of sorted){
    drawWindow(w);
  }

  // draw taskbar on bottom monitor(s) inside left monitor area for demo
  const activeMon = mons[1]; // emulate taskbar anchored to laptop for this demo
  const tbW = activeMon.w - 24, tbH = 46;
  const tbX = activeMon.x + 12, tbY = activeMon.y + activeMon.h - tbH - 12;
  // taskbar background
  ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(10,11,16,0.36)';
  roundRect(ctx, tbX, tbY, tbW, tbH, 12); ctx.fill();
  ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.04)';
  roundRect(ctx, tbX+8, tbY+8, 36, 30, 8); ctx.fill();
  // icons
  ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.06)';
  ctx.font = '12px system-ui';
  ctx.fillText('Taskbar', tbX + 56, tbY + 24);

  // draw virescent tail (glowing blobs)
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
  const now = performance.now();
  for(let i=0;i<virescent.tail.length;i++){
    const p = virescent.tail[i];
    const age = (now - p.t)/1000;
    const fade = clamp(1 - (i/virescent.tail.length) - age*0.4, 0, 1);
    if(fade <= 0) continue;
    const hue = moodHue(virescent.mood) + i*1.6 + Math.sin((i/4)+now/900)*6;
    const alpha = 0.12 * fade * virescent.glow;
    ctx.fillStyle = `hsla(${hue},92%,${40 + fade*18}%,${alpha})`;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, 8 + i*0.7, 0, Math.PI*2);
    ctx.fill();
  }
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'source-over';

  // draw head bloom
  const grd = ctx.createRadialGradient(virescent.x, virescent.y, 0, virescent.x, virescent.y, 58);
  const hue = moodHue(virescent.mood);
  grd.addColorStop(0, `hsla(${hue},95%,92%,${0.9 * virescent.glow})`);
  grd.addColorStop(0.2, `hsla(${hue - 10},90%,72%,${0.35 * virescent.glow})`);
  grd.addColorStop(0.9, `hsla(${hue - 40},65%,36%,${0.06 * virescent.glow})`);
  ctx.fillStyle = grd;
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(virescent.x, virescent.y, 28 + virescent.glow*6, 0, Math.PI*2); ctx.fill();

  // electric filaments (thin strokes)
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
  for(let s=0;s<5;s++){
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(virescent.x, virescent.y);
    for(let k=0;k<5;k++){
      const a = k/5*Math.PI*2 + s*0.9;
      const rx = virescent.x + Math.cos(a + now/400 + s)* (6 + k*1.2);
      const ry = virescent.y + Math.sin(a + now/400 + s)* (6 + k*0.9);
      ctx.lineTo(rx + Math.sin(k*1.7)* (s*0.4), ry + Math.cos(k*1.3)* (s*0.3));
    }
    ctx.strokeStyle = `hsla(${hue + s*6}, 98%, ${60 - s*4}%, ${0.07 * virescent.glow})`;
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.0 - s*0.12;
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // draw glyph (unicode rune) as the head/core
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
  ctx.font = '28px "Noto Sans Symbols 2", system-ui, serif';
  ctx.textAlign = 'center';
  ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
  ctx.fillStyle = `rgba(255,255,255,${0.98 * virescent.glow})`;
  ctx.fillText(virescent.rune, virescent.x + virescent.vx*0.006, virescent.y + virescent.vy*0.006);

  // tiny stroke/orbit glyphs when excited
  if(virescent.dashing || virescent._flashSeq.length){
    for(let i=0;i<3;i++){
      const a = (now/200) + i*2.1;
      const ox = virescent.x + Math.cos(a)* (22 + i*6);
      const oy = virescent.y + Math.sin(a)* (14 + i*5);
      ctx.font = `${12 + i*2}px system-ui`;
      ctx.fillStyle = `hsla(${hue + i*16},95%,68%,${0.9 - i*0.22})`;
      ctx.fillText(RUNES[(i + (Math.floor(now/150)%RUNES.length))%RUNES.length], ox, oy);
    }
  }

  // small label showing mode
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation='source-over';
  ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.06)';
  ctx.font = '12px system-ui';
  ctx.fillText('Mode: ' + virescent.behavior + (virescent.geometryMode!=='none'?(' / '+virescent.geometryMode):''), 14, canvas.height - 18);
}

/* helper for rounded rect */
function roundRect(ctx,x,y,w,h,r){
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.moveTo(x+r, y);
  ctx.arcTo(x+w, y, x+w, y+h, r);
  ctx.arcTo(x+w, y+h, x, y+h, r);
  ctx.arcTo(x, y+h, x, y, r);
  ctx.arcTo(x, y, x+w, y, r);
  ctx.closePath();
}

/* ---------- small UI: clicking windows focuses them ---------- */
/* We allow clicking on window rectangles by pointerdown logic earlier.
   Also allow double-clicking empty area to spawn window on current monitor.
*/
canvas.addEventListener('dblclick', (ev)=>{
  const rect = canvas.getBoundingClientRect();
  const x = ev.clientX - rect.left, y = ev.clientY - rect.top;
  // decide monitor
  const mons = getMonitors();
  let mon = mons[1];
  if(x < mons[0].x + mons[0].w) mon = mons[0];
  spawnWindow({x:x-60,y:y-30,monitor:mon.id});
});

/* ---------- draw windows function ---------- */
function drawWindow(w){
  // window shadow and body
  const corner = 8;
  ctx.save();
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'source-over';
  // window background
  roundRect(ctx, w.x, w.y, w.w, w.h, corner);
  ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(18,20,30,0.88)';
  ctx.fill();
  // title bar
  ctx.fillStyle = w.focused ? 'rgba(255,255,255,0.06)' : 'rgba(255,255,255,0.03)';
  roundRect(ctx, w.x, w.y, w.w, 30, {tl:corner,tr:corner,br:0,bl:0});
  ctx.fill();
  // title text
  ctx.fillStyle = w.focused ? 'rgba(255,255,255,0.95)' : 'rgba(255,255,255,0.7)';
  ctx.font = '13px system-ui';
  ctx.fillText(w.title, w.x + 12, w.y + 19);
  // body content (placeholder)
  ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.03)';
  ctx.fillRect(w.x + 12, w.y + 36, Math.max(40,w.w-24), Math.max(20,w.h-48));

  // small edge highlight to make it attractive
  ctx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.02)';
  ctx.lineWidth = 1;
  ctx.stroke();

  // when focused, draw a "perch" hint for Virescent
  if(w.focused){
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(255,255,255,0.02)';
    ctx.fillRect(w.x + w.w - 66, w.y + 6, 44, 18);
  }

  // z-index effect: a faint glow
  if(w.focused){
    ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
    const g = ctx.createLinearGradient(w.x, w.y, w.x + w.w, w.y + w.h);
    g.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(255,255,255,0.01)');
    g.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(180,220,255,0.01)');
    ctx.fillStyle = g;
    roundRect(ctx, w.x-6, w.y-6, w.w+12, w.h+12, corner+6); ctx.fill();
  }

  ctx.restore();
}

/* ---------- helpers ---------- */
function moodHue(mood){
  switch(mood){
    case 'calm': return 200; // teal
    case 'curious': return 190;
    case 'mischief': return 320; // magenta
    case 'playful': return 40; // warm golden
    default: return 190;
  }
}

/* ---------- initialization ---------- */
spawnWindow({title:'Welcome', monitor:'Laptop', w:380, h:220});
spawnWindow({title:'Visualizer', monitor:'TV', w:420, h:280});
virescent.resetPosition();
virescent.setBehavior('edge');

/* small helper to lerp */
function lerp(a,b,t){return a + (b-a)*t}

/* ---------- keyboard shortcuts (for play) ---------- */
window.addEventListener('keydown', (e)=>{
  if(e.key === '1') document.getElementById('behavior').value = 'edge', virescent.setBehavior('edge');
  if(e.key === '2') document.getElementById('behavior').value = 'wander', virescent.setBehavior('wander');
  if(e.key === '3') document.getElementById('behavior').value = 'geometry', virescent.setBehavior('geometry');
});

/* ---------- small autospawn flavor: when a new window appears it will cause Virescent to inspect ---------- */
const originalSpawn = spawnWindow;
spawnWindow = function(opts){
  const w = originalSpawn(opts);
  // cause quick focus event
  setTimeout(()=>focusWindow(w.id), 80 + Math.random()*160);
  return w;
};

</script>
</body>

Quick notes & what to try next
Drag windows between the left (TV) and right (Laptop) simulated monitors; Virescent will hop and treat them as distinct rooms.

Click a window to focus it — Virescent will dash to inspect. Double-click empty space to spawn a new window at that location.

Change Behavior to and pick a geometry mode to watch it draw spirals, butterflies, or raindrop crawls.geometry

Tweak Mischief and Tail length — great for personality tuning.

Press // to quickly switch behaviors.123

If you love how this moves, I’ll convert the movement & glyph systems into a real overlay next (Electron boilerplate that creates a transparent, click-through window and listens for actual OS window positions). Or we can convert this into a Rainmeter + AHK package so it runs on your desktop without Electron — whichever you prefer.

About Electron, C#, C++, Python
Here’s the quick map:

Electron
• Yes, you’d download it
• Lets us run web-tech (HTML/CSS/JS) as a full desktop program
• Works outside the browser
• Can stay running in the background
• Works on Windows perfectly
• Best for rapid development & maximum visual control (this matches your needs)

Python
• Feels tempting, but visual layers are clunkier
• Harder to get butter-smooth animation
• Not ideal for multi-layer UI overlays
• Possible, but not optimal for this creature

C# (with .NET + WPF)
• Extremely powerful for Windows overlay apps
• We can build a transparent always-on-top window
• Great performance
• More code overhead, but very stable
• Your VS Code setup can handle this just fine

C++ + DirectX/OpenGL
• The nuclear option
• Fastest
• Hardest
• More work than needed for a single sprite entity
• Only worth it if we turn Virescent into a full desktop simulation-engine creature

Given who you are — aesthetic precision, rhythm obsession, visual delight — Electron or C# are the perfect tier.
Electron gets us running sooner, C# gives long-term stability.

🔥 Virescent Prototype v0.3 — Trick Expansion
(This is long, but self-contained. It will run immediately.)

<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="UTF-8" />
<title>Virescent Sprite v0.3</title>
<style>
  html, body {
    margin: 0; padding: 0; overflow: hidden;
    background: #000;
    height: 100%; width: 100%;
  }

  #sprite {
    position: absolute;
    width: 22px; height: 22px;
    border-radius: 50%;
    background: radial-gradient(circle, #9affd8, #00a76f 70%, #004d35 100%);
    box-shadow: 0 0 20px #1affb7, 0 0 40px #1affb7;
    pointer-events: none;
    transform-style: preserve-3d;
  }

  .trail {
    position: absolute;
    color: #66ffc4;
    font-size: 18px;
    opacity: 0.85;
    filter: blur(0.5px);
    pointer-events: none;
    animation: fadeout 1.4s ease forwards;
  }

  @keyframes fadeout {
    0% { opacity: 0.8; transform: scale(1); }
    100% { opacity: 0; transform: scale(0.3); }
  }

  .shine {
    position: absolute;
    width: 8px; height: 80px;
    background: linear-gradient(to bottom, #cafff2 0%, rgba(0,255,180,0) 100%);
    filter: blur(3px);
    pointer-events: none;
    opacity: 0.9;
    transform-origin: center;
    animation: shineFade 0.9s ease-out forwards;
  }

  @keyframes shineFade {
    0% { opacity: 0.9; }
    100% { opacity: 0; transform: scale(0.6); }
  }

  .streak {
    position: absolute;
    width: 60px; height: 4px;
    background: linear-gradient(to right, rgba(0,255,180,0), #88ffe0, rgba(0,255,180,0));
    filter: blur(2px);
    pointer-events: none;
    opacity: 0.85;
    animation: streakFade 0.6s ease-out forwards;
  }

  @keyframes streakFade {
    0% { opacity: 0.85; }
    100% { opacity: 0; width: 90px; }
  }
</style>
</head>

<body>
<div id="sprite"></div>

<script>
const sprite = document.getElementById("sprite");
let x = window.innerWidth / 2;
let y = window.innerHeight / 2;

let vx = (Math.random() - 0.5) * 2;
let vy = (Math.random() - 0.5) * 2;

const glyphs = ["᚛", "᚜", "ᛝ", "۞", "₪", "✧", "ᚾ", "߷", "౨", "ᔑ"];

let mood = "calm";
let moodTimer = 0;
let mouseX = x, mouseY = y;

// gentle mouse tracking bursts
document.addEventListener("mousemove", e => {
  mouseX = e.clientX;
  mouseY = e.clientY;
});

// main animation loop
function loop() {
  const dx = mouseX - x;
  const dy = mouseY - y;
  const distToMouse = Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy);

  moodTimer--;
  if (moodTimer <= 0) pickMood();

  // occasional curiosity toward the mouse
  if (mood === "curious" && Math.random() < 0.02) {
    vx += (dx / distToMouse) * 1.2;
    vy += (dy / distToMouse) * 1.2;
  }

  // playful = more randomness
  if (mood === "playful") {
    vx += (Math.random() - 0.5) * 0.2;
    vy += (Math.random() - 0.5) * 0.2;

    if (Math.random() < 0.01) spawnShine(x, y);
  }

  // mischief = speed bursts
  if (mood === "mischief") {
    if (Math.random() < 0.02) spawnStreak(x, y);
    if (Math.random() < 0.005) spawnLongGlyphTrail(x, y);
  }

  // wall hugging: glow behind windows effect
  if (x < 20 || x > window.innerWidth - 20) {
    if (Math.random() < 0.03) spawnShine(x, y);
  }
  if (y < 20 || y > window.innerHeight - 20) {
    if (Math.random() < 0.03) spawnShine(x, y);
  }

  x += vx;
  y += vy;

  // soft screen edges
  if (x < 0) { x = 0; vx *= -1; }
  if (x > window.innerWidth) { x = window.innerWidth; vx *= -1; }
  if (y < 0) { y = 0; vy *= -1; }
  if (y > window.innerHeight) { y = window.innerHeight; vy *= -1; }

  sprite.style.left = x - 11 + "px";
  sprite.style.top = y - 11 + "px";

  if (Math.random() < 0.15) spawnGlyphTrail(x, y);

  requestAnimationFrame(loop);
}

function pickMood() {
  const choices = ["calm", "curious", "playful", "mischief"];
  mood = choices[Math.floor(Math.random()*choices.length)];
  moodTimer = 200 + Math.random()*400;
}

function spawnGlyphTrail(px, py) {
  const g = document.createElement("div");
  g.className = "trail";
  g.style.left = px + (Math.random()*20-10) + "px";
  g.style.top = py + (Math.random()*20-10) + "px";
  g.textContent = glyphs[Math.floor(Math.random()*glyphs.length)];
  document.body.appendChild(g);
  setTimeout(()=>g.remove(),1400);
}

// rare long tendrils that wrap across large areas
function spawnLongGlyphTrail(px, py) {
  for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {
    setTimeout(() => {
      spawnGlyphTrail(px + (i*20*(Math.random()-0.5)),
                      py + (i*20*(Math.random()-0.5)));
    }, i * 30);
  }
}

function spawnShine(px, py) {
  const s = document.createElement("div");
  s.className = "shine";
  s.style.left = (px - 4) + "px";
  s.style.top = (py - 40) + "px";
  s.style.transform = `rotate(${Math.random()*180}deg)`;
  document.body.appendChild(s);
  setTimeout(()=>s.remove(),900);
}

function spawnStreak(px, py) {
  const s = document.createElement("div");
  s.className = "streak";
  s.style.left = (px - 30) + "px";
  s.style.top = (py - 2) + "px";
  s.style.transform = `rotate(${Math.random()*360}deg)`;
  document.body.appendChild(s);
  setTimeout(()=>s.remove(),600);
}

loop();
</script>

</body>
</html>

function createWindow() {

1. Find Where Electron Actually Installed

C:\Users\brigi\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules
Inside that folder, you should see an directory.electron

npm bin -g
You’ll get something like:

C:\Users\brigi\AppData\Roaming\npm
Inside that folder, there should be some files:

electron
electron.cmd
Windows only runs programs that live in your PATH.
If this folder isn’t in PATH, PowerShell will swear Electron doesn’t exist.

2. Check Your PATH

echo $env:PATH
Look for:

C:\Users\brigi\AppData\Roaming\npm
If it’s not there, that’s the problem.

Windows Search: type Environment Variables

Click Edit the system environment variables

Button Environment Variables…

In User variables, double-click Path

Click New

Add the folder shown by , for example:npm bin -g

C:\Users\brigi\AppData\Roaming\npm

ChatGPT said:
Your shell is telling you the truth, but in that annoying, cryptic way computers love. The short version: npm v10+ removed npm bin -g. It used to exist; it no longer does. The command simply isn’t a thing anymore.

The good news is that you can still get the global binary path. The trick is to ask npm for its global prefix, then append the standard folder yourself. It’s a bit like finding a wizard’s tower by locating the forest first.bin

Try this:

npm config get prefix
On Windows, this usually returns something like:

C:\Users\brigi\AppData\Roaming\npm
That directory is your global bin directory already. Node on Windows doesn’t separate from ; the executables live right in that folder.binprefix

So, effectively, your global bin path is:

C:\Users\brigi\AppData\Roaming\npm
You can confirm by checking whether it contains a bunch of wrappers pointing to packages you've installed globally..cmd

If you ever need to query things like this in the future, npm’s config system is the little crystal ball that still works. You can even ask it:

npm config list
That gives you a full map of the territory.

ChatGPT said:
Your system is playing a cosmic joke: npm swears Electron is installed globally, but Windows resolutely insists the command does not exist. When that happens, there's only one explanation in the labyrinth: Electron never created a global CLI shim.

And that’s the punchline.
Electron doesn’t create an exe/command called .
Not anymore.
Not globally.electron

The “global Electron install” is a bit of misleading lore from ancient times. Modern Electron shifted away from that model. Global installation still downloads the package into , but it does not generate a runnable global command.node_modules

In other words, the phantom hunt for is doomed. You could search that entire directory with a miner's headlamp, and you will find no such file.electron.cmdnpm

Here’s the actual truth, plain and slightly absurd:

• Electron is meant to be installed locally inside each project.
• You run it with:

npx electron .
or

npm run start
(with a script defined).

That’s why no amount of PATH tweaking resurrects the command—because Windows isn’t blocking it. The command does not exist.

Here’s how to summon your glowing UI sprite in real world practice:

Make a project folder

mkdir plasma-sprite
cd plasma-sprite
npm init -y
Install Electron locally

npm install electron --save-dev
Run Electron using npx

npx electron .
(Optional, nicer) Edit package.json and add:

"scripts": {
  "start": "electron ."
}
Then you just do:

npm start
This is the intended modern workflow.

If you're imagining Electron as a kind of global engine running under your whole OS like a background daemon for your shimmering little plasma bug… this approach still works. You simply make one Electron project that launches the UI sprite and let that app run quietly in the background.

You do not launch Electron itself globally—you launch your Electron app.

If you want, I can scaffold the entire tiny “sprite drifting under your windows” app next.

function createWindow() {
  const win = new BrowserWindow({

    transparent: true,
    frame: false,
    alwaysOnTop: true,
    resizable: false,
    hasShadow: false,
    webPreferences: {
      nodeIntegration: true,
      contextIsolation: false
    }
  });

  win.setIgnoreMouseEvents(true);
  win.loadFile('index.html');
}

app.whenReady().then(createWindow);

<!-- save as virescent-demo.html and open in a browser -->
<!doctype html>
<html>
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8"/>
<title>Virescent — energy-glyph demo</title>
<style>
  html,body{height:100%;margin:0;background:#0b0b10;overflow:hidden;font-family:system-ui;}
  #ui {
    position:absolute;left:8%;top:8%;width:84%;height:76%;
    border-radius:10px;border:2px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.05);
    box-shadow: 0 20px 60px rgba(0,0,0,0.75), inset 0 0 30px rgba(255,255,255,0.02);
    backdrop-filter: blur(6px);
  }
  #controls{position:fixed;right:12px;top:12px;color:#dfe6ff;opacity:.95}
  button{display:block;margin:6px;padding:6px 10px;background:#131321;border:1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.06);color:#e6ecff;border-radius:6px}
  canvas{position:absolute;left:0;top:0;pointer-events:none}
  .hint{font-size:12px;color:#9aa7ff;opacity:.7;margin-top:8px}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<div id="ui"></div>
<canvas id="c"></canvas>

<div id="controls">
  <button id="modeBtn">Mode: edge-trace</button>
  <button id="patternBtn">Pattern: none</button>
  <button id="morseBtn">Morse: off</button>
  <div class="hint">Click inside the framed area to simulate "task switch" dash.</div>
</div>

<script>
const canvas = document.getElementById('c');
const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d', { alpha: true });
const ui = document.getElementById('ui');

function resize(){
  canvas.width = window.innerWidth;
  canvas.height = window.innerHeight;
}
window.addEventListener('resize', resize);
resize();

/* --- CONFIG --- */
let mode = 'edge'; // edge | wander | pattern
let pattern = 'none'; // none | butterfly | fractal
let morse = false;

/* Glyph state */
const glyph = {
  x: 0, y: 0,
  vx: 0, vy: 0,
  speed: 650,         // px per second nominal
  tail: [],           // points for tail
  tailLen: 22,
  glow: 1.0,
  modeTimer: 0,
  dashing: false
};

/* Window-frame bounds to trace (simulate active window) */
function getFrameRect() {
  // Use the ui element as our simulated window
  const r = ui.getBoundingClientRect();
  return { x: r.left, y: r.top, w: r.width, h: r.height };
}

/* helpers */
function lerp(a,b,t){return a + (b-a)*t}
function distance(a,b){return Math.hypot(a.x-b.x,a.y-b.y)}
function clamp(v,a,b){return Math.max(a, Math.min(b, v));}

/* Path builders */
function perimeterPoints(rect, density=120){
  const p = [];
  const {x,y,w,h} = rect;
  // build points around the rectangle clockwise
  const seg = Math.round(density/4);
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x + i*(w/seg), y:y});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x+w, y:y + i*(h/seg)});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x + w - i*(w/seg), y:y+h});
  for(let i=0;i<seg;i++) p.push({x:x, y:y + h - i*(h/seg)});
  return p;
}

/* Patterns */
function butterflyPath(center, t) {
  // parametric "butterfly-ish" curve (stylized)
  const pts = [];
  for(let i=0;i<200;i++){
    const u = i/200 * Math.PI*2;
    const r = Math.sin(2*u)*1.6 + Math.sin(6*u)*0.6;
    const x = center.x + Math.cos(u + t)*80*r;
    const y = center.y + Math.sin(u + t)*60*r;
    pts.push({x,y});
  }
  return pts;
}
function fractalPath(center, depth=4) {
  // simple recursive-ish swirling points (not a full fractal; just flavor)
  const pts = [];
  const max = 400;
  for(let i=0;i<max;i++){
    const a = i/max * Math.PI*8;
    const r = (i/max)*220 * Math.sin(a*0.3) * 0.9;
    pts.push({x:center.x + Math.cos(a)*r, y:center.y + Math.sin(a)*r});
  }
  return pts;
}

/* Move mechanics */
let last = performance.now();
function step(now){
  const dt = Math.min(0.06,(now - last)/1000);
  last = now;
  const rect = getFrameRect();

  // behavior switching (idle wander vs edge trace vs pattern)
  glyph.modeTimer += dt;
  if(mode === 'edge'){
    // follow a point moving along perimeter
    const perim = perimeterPoints(rect, 200);
    const t = (now/2000) % perim.length;
    const idx = Math.floor(t);
    const target = perim[idx];
    followTarget(target, dt);
  } else if(mode === 'wander'){
    // random drift inside rect, prefer edges
    if(!glyph._wanderTarget || distance(glyph, glyph._wanderTarget)<6 || glyph.modeTimer>3.5){
      glyph._wanderTarget = {
        x: rect.x + Math.random()*(rect.w),
        y: rect.y + Math.random()*(rect.h)
      };
      glyph.modeTimer = 0;
    }
    followTarget(glyph._wanderTarget, dt);
  } else if(mode === 'pattern'){
    // follow pattern path centered on rect center
    const center = {x: rect.x + rect.w/2, y: rect.y + rect.h/2};
    const t = now/1200;
    let pts = (pattern==='butterfly') ? butterflyPath(center,t) :
              (pattern==='fractal') ? fractalPath(center) : [];
    if(pts.length){
      const idx = Math.floor((t*0.2)%pts.length);
      const target = pts[idx];
      followTarget(target, dt);
    }
  }

  // tail management
  glyph.tail.unshift({x:glyph.x, y:glyph.y, t:now});
  if(glyph.tail.length > glyph.tailLen) glyph.tail.pop();

  // glow decay
  glyph.glow = lerp(glyph.glow, glyph.dashing ? 2.0 : 0.9, dt*4);

  render();
  requestAnimationFrame(step);
}

function followTarget(target, dt){
  const dx = target.x - glyph.x;
  const dy = target.y - glyph.y;
  const dist = Math.hypot(dx,dy) || 1;
  const maxspeed = glyph.dashing ? glyph.speed*3 : glyph.speed;
  const desiredVx = (dx/dist) * maxspeed;
  const desiredVy = (dy/dist) * maxspeed;
  // smooth velocity (lag)
  glyph.vx = lerp(glyph.vx, desiredVx, dt*6);
  glyph.vy = lerp(glyph.vy, desiredVy, dt*6);
  glyph.x += glyph.vx * dt;
  glyph.y += glyph.vy * dt;
}

/* draw glow tail + head */
function render(){
  ctx.clearRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
  // faint full-screen noise vignette (tasteful)
  // draw tail as trail of fading plasma
  const now = performance.now();
  for(let i=0;i<glyph.tail.length;i++){
    const p = glyph.tail[i];
    const age = (now - p.t)/1000;
    const fade = clamp(1 - (i/glyph.tail.length) - age*0.3, 0, 1);
    if(fade<=0) continue;
    // color ramps
    const hue = 190 + i*1.2 + Math.sin((i/6)+now/800)*6;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
    ctx.fillStyle = ;
    ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, 10 + i*0.6, 0, Math.PI*2);
    ctx.fill();
  }

  // drawing the "comma" head and electric filaments
  ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'lighter';
  // filament noise
  for(let s=0;s<6;s++){
    const jitter = (s/6)*6;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(glyph.x, glyph.y);
    for(let k=0;k<6;k++){
      const a = k/6*Math.PI*2 + s*0.9;
      const rx = glyph.x + Math.cos(a+performance.now()/400 + s)* (6 + k*1.2);
      const ry = glyph.y + Math.sin(a+performance.now()/400 + s)* (6 + k*0.9);
      ctx.lineTo(rx + Math.sin(k*1.7)*jitter, ry + Math.cos(k*1.3)*jitter);
    }
    ctx.strokeStyle = ;
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.2 - s*0.12;
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // head bloom
  const grd = ctx.createRadialGradient(glyph.x, glyph.y, 0, glyph.x, glyph.y, 44);
  grd.addColorStop(0, );
  grd.addColorStop(0.25, );
  grd.addColorStop(0.8, );
  ctx.fillStyle = grd;
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(glyph.x, glyph.y, 18, 0, Math.PI*2); ctx.fill();

  // sharp core
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.fillStyle = ;
  ctx.ellipse(glyph.x + glyph.vx*0.008, glyph.y + glyph.vy*0.008, 6, 8, Math.atan2(glyph.vy,glyph.vx), 0, Math.PI*2);
  ctx.fill();

  // optional morse-like blink (a little flash on the head)
  if(morse){
    const ms = Math.floor(performance.now()/180) % 8;
    if(ms<2){
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.fillStyle = ;
      ctx.arc(glyph.x, glyph.y, 36, 0, Math.PI*2);
      ctx.fill();
    }
  }
}

/* UI interactions to simulate task switching and mode */
document.getElementById('modeBtn').addEventListener('click', ()=>{
  if(mode==='edge'){ mode='wander'; document.getElementById('modeBtn').textContent='Mode: wander'; }
  else if(mode==='wander'){ mode='pattern'; document.getElementById('modeBtn').textContent='Mode: pattern'; }
  else { mode='edge'; document.getElementById('modeBtn').textContent='Mode: edge-trace'; }
});

document.getElementById('patternBtn').addEventListener('click', ()=>{
  if(pattern==='none'){ pattern='butterfly'; document.getElementById('patternBtn').textContent='Pattern: butterfly';}
  else if(pattern==='butterfly'){ pattern='fractal'; document.getElementById('patternBtn').textContent='Pattern: fractal';}
  else { pattern='none'; document.getElementById('patternBtn').textContent='Pattern: none';}
});

document.getElementById('morseBtn').addEventListener('click', ()=>{
  morse = !morse;
  document.getElementById('morseBtn').textContent = ;
});

/* clicking the frame simulates "task switch" dash */
ui.addEventListener('click', (ev)=>{
  glyph.dashing = true;
  glyph.modeTimer = 0;
  setTimeout(()=>glyph.dashing = false, 240);
});

/* initialize glyph at a corner */
const r0 = getFrameRect();
glyph.x = r0.x + 6;
glyph.y = r0.y + 6;
glyph.tail = [];
requestAnimationFrame(step);
</script>
</body>
</html>

    display:flex;gap:6px;align-items:center;
  }
  .appBtn{padding:6px 10px;background:rgba(255,255,255,0.02);border-radius:8px;border:1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.03);font-size:13px}
  .smallMuted{font-size:12px;color:rgba(255,255,255,0.32)}
  footer{position:fixed;left:12px;bottom:12px;color:rgba(255,255,255,0.12);font-size:12px}
</style>
</head>
